> AGENT

No remaining findings in the reviewed payment/grants cutover after the follow-up fixes.

I fixed the three issues from the review:
- producer handoff recovery is now retried after startup if a terminal payment was recovered before the producer cog was available in [payment_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_cog.py#L121)
- recipient authorization is enforced in the service layer, not just the Discord button handler, in [payment_service.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_service.py#L168)
- admin release from `manual_hold` can no longer set a payment to `confirmed`; it is limited to `failed` or staying held in [db_handler.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/common/db_handler.py#L1952), [tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/tools.py#L536), and [agent.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/agent.py#L60)

Coverage was updated for all three in [test_scheduler.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/tests/test_scheduler.py#L419), [test_social_publish_service.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/tests/test_social_publish_service.py#L466), and [test_social_route_tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/tests/test_social_route_tools.py#L345). Verification is green: `PYTHONPATH=. PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 pytest -q` passed with `36 passed`.

Residual risk is mostly operational now: route config still needs to be set correctly in production, and staging should still verify the real Discord confirmation destination and admin hold/release flow.

> DEVELOPER

SO how do i create a new payment? Does the bot have a way to do this with the admin?

> AGENT

I’m checking the actual admin surfaces before answering, because there’s a difference between “the subsystem supports it” and “the bot already exposes a tool for it.”

> AGENT

Not yet.

Current state:
- The bot can manage payment infrastructure from admin chat:
  - `create_payment_route`
  - `list_payment_routes`
  - `list_wallets`
  - `list_payments`
  - `get_payment_status`
  - `retry_payment`
  - `hold_payment`
  - `release_payment`
  - `cancel_payment`
  You can see those in [agent.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/agent.py#L51) and [tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/tools.py#L333).

- But there is no admin-chat tool today to create a brand-new payment request manually.
- New payments are currently created by producer code calling `PaymentService.request_payment(...)`, and the only wired producer right now is grants in [grants_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/grants/grants_cog.py#L568) and [grants_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/grants/grants_cog.py#L680).

So for microgrants:
- approve grant
- applicant sends wallet
- grants cog creates the test payment
- after test confirms, grants cog creates the final payment

If you want arbitrary admin-created payments, the next clean step is to add a dedicated admin tool like `create_payment(...)` or `request_payment(...)` that:
- takes `producer`, `producer_ref`, `recipient_discord_id`, `wallet_id` or wallet address, `amount_usd`, `is_test`
- resolves the route
- creates the ledger row through `PaymentService`
- optionally posts the confirmation prompt immediately

That tool does not exist yet. If you want, I can add it next.

> AGENT

Yes. I started a real heavy `megaplan` for this and the first draft got correctly rejected. The active plan is [admin-triggered-payments-let-20260410-1448](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/.megaplan/plans/admin-triggered-payments-let-20260410-1448), and the critique/gate both caught the main mistake: the draft tried to make this an `admin supplies wallet` tool, which is not your design. The critique is in [critique_output.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/.megaplan/plans/admin-triggered-payments-let-20260410-1448/critique_output.json) and the rejection is in [gate.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/.megaplan/plans/admin-triggered-payments-let-20260410-1448/gate.json). I’ve already kicked off a revise pass to correct that.

The corrected plan should be this:

**Megaplan**
Build this as a new admin-triggered producer on top of the shared fail-closed payments subsystem, not as a special-case bypass.

1. Add an admin payment intent layer.
- Create a persisted `admin_payment_intents` table keyed by `intent_id`.
- Store `guild_id`, `source_channel_id`, `source_thread_id`, `admin_user_id`, `recipient_user_id`, `amount_usd`, `reason`, `status`, `wallet_id`, `wallet_address_snapshot`, `payment_request_id_test`, `payment_request_id_final`, `confirmation_message_id`, `last_error`, `metadata`.
- This is the missing async state store for “admin requested payment, now waiting for recipient wallet reply / test confirmation / final confirmation.”

2. Add an admin initiation tool, but do not require wallet input.
- New admin tool like `initiate_payment_intent(recipient_user_id, amount_usd, reason?)`.
- Tool must capture the originating guild/channel/thread context from the admin chat call path.
- If a verified Solana wallet already exists for that Banodoco user, the intent can move straight to test-payment creation.
- If no wallet exists, the bot posts in the originating channel/thread tagging the intended recipient and asks them to reply with their Solana wallet there.

3. Add a deterministic recipient-reply handler.
- Do not rely on the general admin-chat LLM path for wallet collection.
- Add a dedicated listener in [admin_chat_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/admin_chat_cog.py) or a small payment-intent cog that intercepts replies for open admin payment intents before the normal admin/member permission gate.
- Only accept wallet replies from the exact `recipient_user_id` tied to the intent.
- Validate wallet format exactly as the grants flow does, then upsert into the secure wallet table and attach the wallet to the intent.

4. Reuse the shared payment subsystem for money movement.
- Once wallet is known, create a shared `payment_request` for the fixed tiny test payment.
- Producer should be something like `admin_chat` so existing producer handoff logic can find the cog cleanly.
- On test confirmation, create the final payment request through `PaymentService`; do not send funds directly from admin-chat code.

5. Keep the final confirmation recipient-driven and fail-closed.
- Confirmation must only count if it comes from the intended `recipient_user_id`, in the intended channel/thread, for an active intent/payment pair.
- Since you specifically want message-based confirmation, add a narrow confirmation parser:
  - first gate on recipient identity and active pending final payment
  - then run an LLM classification only on that recipient’s message
  - require an explicit positive semantic like “yes I received it” / “confirmed”
  - ambiguous output must stay pending, never advance
- I would still strongly consider a button as the primary confirm path with LLM as fallback, because free-text financial confirmation is higher risk.

6. Lock down wallet storage and access.
- Wallets should remain in the secure wallet table, not in the admin intent row as editable source of truth.
- Intent row can store a snapshot/reference, but canonical wallet should live in the secured wallet store with restricted access.
- Payment tables and wallet tables must stay excluded from broad admin-chat querying.

7. Add explicit context plumbing.
- The current critique correctly found that admin tool execution does not reliably inject `channel_id` / thread context.
- Fix the admin chat execution path so admin tool calls always carry:
  - `guild_id`
  - `channel_id`
  - `thread_id` when applicable
  - `admin_user_id`
- DM initiation is out of scope for now; guild-channel initiation only.

8. Handle permission-gate conflicts.
- The current member access gate in admin chat would block the recipient’s normal reply path.
- Add a narrow bypass only for messages that match an open admin payment intent for that exact user in that exact channel/thread.
- Do not widen general bot access for members beyond that.

9. Add clear statuses.
- Suggested intent statuses:
  - `pending_wallet`
  - `wallet_received`
  - `test_payment_requested`
  - `awaiting_test_result`
  - `awaiting_final_confirmation`
  - `final_payment_requested`
  - `completed`
  - `manual_hold`
  - `failed`
  - `cancelled`

10. Test the dangerous paths first.
- Non-recipient replies are ignored.
- Wrong-channel replies are ignored.
- Ambiguous confirmation text does not trigger payout.
- Crash after test payment submit does not double-send.
- Crash after final submit does not double-send.
- Restart while awaiting wallet/confirmation still resumes correctly.
- Existing wallet path skips wallet prompt and starts at test payment.

**Main Concerns**
- The highest-risk part is the LLM-confirmed free-text final confirmation. I would only ship that if it is identity-gated first and fail-closed on ambiguity.
- The admin tool must not take `wallet_address` as the primary path. That was the first-plan mistake.
- This needs a persisted intent table. Without it, the async “ask recipient and resume later” flow is not real.
- The recipient reply path cannot go through the normal member-permission gate unchanged.
- Channel/thread context must be injected explicitly into admin tool execution, or the bot won’t know where to ask for wallet/confirmation.

**Questions**
- Should a wallet provided in this flow automatically become that user’s canonical Banodoco Solana wallet for future payments? My assumption is yes.
- Do you want the final confirmation message to happen in the same source thread/channel, or can it go to a configured payments channel once the wallet is known? My assumption from your note is same originating channel/thread.
- For LLM confirmation, do you want a strict explicit-confirmation requirement, or should softer statements like “looks good” count? My recommendation is strict only.

If you want, I can keep the megaplan running to a clean revised artifact and then hand you the final `plan_v2`/`final.md`, but the design above is the right shape.

> DEVELOPER

Describe the process to me concretely and specifically what specifically happens and who gets permission of what and who can do what. Thank you.

> AGENT

Here’s the concrete intended flow.

**Actors**
- `Admin`: can initiate a payment intent by tagging or DM-ing the admin-chat bot in a guild channel/thread. For now, guild channel/thread only.
- `Recipient`: the specific Banodoco/Discord user the admin is trying to pay.
- `Bot`: creates the intent, asks for wallet if needed, sends test payment, interprets recipient confirmation, and only then sends final payment.
- `PaymentService`: owns the actual payment state machine and submission logic.
- `DB`: stores the admin payment intent, secure wallet record, and payment ledger rows.

**Step-by-Step**
1. `Admin` mentions the bot in a guild channel or thread and says something like:
   - “pay @alice $25 for X”
2. The admin-chat system parses that into:
   - `recipient_user_id`
   - `amount_usd`
   - optional `reason`
   - `guild_id`
   - `channel_id`
   - `thread_id` if present
   - `admin_user_id`
3. The bot creates an `admin_payment_intent` row with status like `pending_wallet` or `wallet_ready`.
4. The bot checks whether that exact recipient already has a stored verified Solana wallet in the secure wallet table.
5. If wallet exists:
   - the bot moves straight to creating the tiny fixed test payment through the shared `PaymentService`.
6. If wallet does not exist:
   - the bot replies in the same originating channel/thread
   - it tags the intended recipient only
   - it asks them to reply with their Solana wallet
   - the intent stays in `pending_wallet`

**Wallet Collection Permissions**
- Only the intended `recipient_user_id` is allowed to satisfy the wallet request.
- If anyone else replies with a wallet:
   - ignored for progression
   - optionally bot says “only @recipient can provide the wallet for this payment”
- The wallet reply handler must bypass normal member admin-chat gating only for:
   - an open payment intent
   - in the matching channel/thread
   - from the exact intended recipient
- That bypass should not grant the user general admin-chat access.

**Wallet Handling**
- Recipient replies with a Solana wallet.
- Bot validates wallet format.
- If invalid:
   - bot asks the same user to try again
   - no state advances
- If valid:
   - bot upserts that wallet into the secure Banodoco wallet table for that user
   - links the wallet to the admin payment intent
   - moves intent to `wallet_ready`
   - creates the tiny fixed test payment

**Test Payment**
- Bot creates a `payment_request` with:
   - producer `admin_chat`
   - recipient wallet
   - intended Discord recipient
   - test amount
- Shared payment system sends the test payment fail-closed.
- If test payment fails or becomes ambiguous:
   - move to `manual_hold` or `failed`
   - notify admin in the originating channel/thread
   - do not create final payment

**After Test Payment**
- If test payment confirms successfully:
   - bot posts in the same originating channel/thread
   - tags the intended recipient
   - asks them to confirm they received it
   - intent moves to `awaiting_final_confirmation`

**Final Confirmation Permissions**
- Only the intended recipient can confirm.
- Only messages in the intended originating channel/thread count.
- Only while there is an open `awaiting_final_confirmation` intent/payment pair.
- Admin cannot impersonate this confirmation through normal flow.
- Anyone else replying is ignored for payout progression.

**How Confirmation Works**
You said you want LLM reading here. The safe way is:

1. Hard gate first:
   - exact recipient user
   - exact channel/thread
   - exact open payment intent
2. Only then run classification on that recipient’s message.
3. Allowed result must be something like:
   - `confirmed_received`
   - `not_confirmed`
   - `ambiguous`
4. Only `confirmed_received` advances.
5. `ambiguous` stays pending.
6. `not_confirmed` stays pending or can move to manual review depending on content.

That means “yes I got it” can work, but vague text like “ok” or “cool” should not.

**Final Payment**
- After positive recipient confirmation:
   - bot creates the final `payment_request`
   - shared `PaymentService` sends it
- If final payment confirms:
   - bot posts success in the originating channel/thread
   - intent moves to `completed`
- If final payment fails or is ambiguous:
   - move to `manual_hold` or `failed`
   - notify admin
   - never auto-double-send

**Who Can Do What**
- `Admin` can:
   - initiate payment intent
   - see status
   - use admin controls like hold/retry/cancel where policy allows
- `Recipient` can:
   - provide wallet for their own pending intent
   - confirm receipt for their own pending intent
- `Recipient` cannot:
   - initiate admin payments
   - confirm someone else’s payment
   - provide wallet for someone else’s payment
- `Other members` can:
   - talk in the thread, but their messages do not progress the payment
- `Bot` can:
   - ask for wallet
   - validate wallet
   - store wallet securely
   - send test payment
   - classify recipient confirmation
   - send final payment only after valid confirmation
- `PaymentService` can:
   - move ledger states
   - submit chain txs
   - recover after restart
   - freeze ambiguous cases

**Security / Permission Boundaries**
- Canonical wallet lives in the secure wallet table, not in chat.
- Payment rows and wallet rows stay excluded from broad admin-chat data querying.
- Private keys never touch the DB.
- No automatic resend after a tx submission unless chain says the tx definitely failed.
- Any ambiguity becomes `manual_hold`.
- The member-permission bypass is only for the narrow “reply to open payment intent” case.

**What This Means Operationally**
Best-case path:
1. Admin says “pay @alice $25”
2. Bot checks wallet
3. If missing, bot asks Alice for wallet
4. Alice replies with wallet
5. Bot stores wallet
6. Bot sends tiny test payment
7. Bot asks Alice to confirm receipt
8. Alice confirms in-thread
9. Bot sends final payment
10. Bot posts completion

Fail-closed path:
1. Wrong user replies
2. ambiguous confirmation
3. invalid wallet
4. RPC ambiguity
5. restart mid-flow

In all of those, the system pauses or holds. It does not guess and does not auto-send the final payment.

If you want, I can turn this into a precise state machine table next: `status`, `allowed actor`, `allowed action`, `next status`.

Yes. Concrete answers:

1. Can they pay in `SOL`?
- `Test` payments: yes, explicitly. The shared system uses a fixed `PAYMENT_TEST_AMOUNT_SOL` for test sends in [payment_service.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_service.py#L19).
- `Final` payments: not as a first-class exact-SOL input right now. The current shared service expects `amount_usd` for non-test payments and converts that to SOL using the live token price in [payment_service.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_service.py#L92).
- So today the system is basically: `final payout amount is USD-denominated, paid out in SOL`.

2. If you initiate a payment and they already provided their address, can the agent pick that up and use it?
- In principle, yes, because the canonical wallet store already exists in `wallet_registry` and the shared payment system can use a `wallet_id` or wallet address from there: [payments.sql](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/sql/payments.sql#L13), [db_handler.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/common/db_handler.py#L1467), [payment_service.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_service.py#L77).
- But the `admin-triggered payment intent` flow itself is not implemented yet. That’s exactly what the megaplan is for.
- So: `wallet reuse is supported by the underlying system`, but `admin mention -> auto-find stored wallet -> continue` is still planned work.

3. Is payment confirmation definitely locked down?
- In the shared payment layer: yes, reasonably tightly.
- The button path checks the exact intended Discord recipient in [payment_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_cog.py#L50).
- More importantly, the service layer also enforces the intended recipient, so a caller cannot bypass the button check and confirm as someone else unless it is an explicit privileged override path: [payment_service.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_service.py#L168).
- For the planned message-based confirmation flow: it is not implemented yet, so I would not call that “definitely locked down” yet. The plan is to lock it first by `recipient identity + channel/thread + active intent`, and only then run LLM classification.

4. If the bot is down, can it pick up messages later? Are we doing that robustly?
- For `payment execution state`: yes, reasonably robustly. On startup, `PaymentCog` re-registers pending confirmation views and recovers inflight payments from DB state in [payment_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_cog.py#L116), [payment_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_cog.py#L149), and [db_handler.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/common/db_handler.py#L2001).
- For `free-text recipient messages while the bot is offline`: no, not yet in the robust way you’re asking about. There is no implemented admin-payment-intent store plus message cursor/replay system for “which recipient confirmation/wallet messages were missed while bot was down.”
- Current admin chat sees live message events and recent context, but it does not maintain a robust offline-read pointer for this planned payment workflow: [admin_chat_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/admin_chat_cog.py#L189).
- So the honest answer is:
  - `payment ledger recovery`: yes
  - `missed free-text wallet/confirmation message recovery`: not implemented yet

5. Is the wallet data stored securely?
- Reasonably, yes, with caveats.
- Wallets are stored in `wallet_registry`, not in generic chat history or payment row source-of-truth: [payments.sql](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/sql/payments.sql#L13).
- RLS is enabled and `anon` / `authenticated` access is revoked for wallet and payment tables: [payments.sql](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/sql/payments.sql#L192).
- Generic admin-table querying excludes these sensitive tables: [test_social_route_tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/tests/test_social_route_tools.py#L286).
- Admin tools that list wallets/payments redact wallet addresses rather than exposing full values broadly.
- Caveat: wallet addresses are still stored in plaintext in the DB, because they have to be usable operationally. That is normal, but it means this is `secured access`, not `cryptographic secrecy`.

So the short version:
- `SOL exact final amounts`: not yet, current final path is USD -> SOL
- `reuse existing stored wallet`: supported underneath, not yet wired for admin-triggered flow
- `confirmation locked down`: yes for current shared button/service flow, not yet for planned LLM message-confirm flow
- `offline missed-message recovery`: no, not yet for the planned admin free-text workflow
- `wallet storage secure`: reasonably yes, with DB/RLS/redaction protections

The biggest gap is #4. If you want this admin-triggered message-based flow to be robust, the megaplan needs to include a persisted `admin_payment_intents` table plus explicit restart reconciliation for missed wallet/confirmation messages.

> AGENT

Yes. Concretely:

1. `SOL`-denominated amounts
- I agree. The clean change is: make `amount_token` / `SOL` the canonical amount everywhere in the payment flow, and treat USD as optional reference metadata only.
- Right now the shared payment system stores both `amount_token` and optional `amount_usd`, but non-test payments are created from `amount_usd` and converted at request time in [payment_service.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_service.py#L92).
- I would flip that for the new admin flow:
  - admin says `pay @user 1.25 SOL`
  - intent stores `requested_amount_sol=1.25`
  - test payment stays fixed tiny SOL
  - final payment uses that exact SOL amount
  - if some upstream producer still starts from USD, convert once, snapshot it, then store/pay/display only SOL from that point forward
- That is simpler and safer because the recipient is confirming the exact amount they will receive.

2. Can the agent navigate the thread / use a replied-to wallet message?
- Yes, partially already.
- Admin chat currently passes thread/channel context, reply target, and the last 10 recent messages into the agent in [admin_chat_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/admin_chat_cog.py#L280) and [admin_chat_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/admin_chat_cog.py#L292).
- So if you reply to a message containing a wallet, the agent can see that `replied_to` content.
- For the payment flow, I would not leave that entirely to the LLM. I’d add deterministic extraction:
  - if the admin’s message is a reply
  - inspect the replied-to message text
  - if it contains one valid Solana address, use it
  - if not, fall back to stored wallet or ask the recipient
- So: yes, the bot can navigate that context, and the robust version should explicitly parse the replied-to message before asking anyone anything.

3. What happens to the button after it’s pressed?
- It does not disappear.
- It gets disabled and the message is edited in place in [payment_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_cog.py#L66).
- Only the intended recipient can press it:
  - UI check in [payment_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_cog.py#L50)
  - service-layer enforcement in [payment_service.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_service.py#L168)
- So a wrong user pressing it does not queue the payment.

4. Does the button time out?
- No normal timeout.
- The view is created with `timeout=None` in [payment_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_cog.py#L25), so it is a persistent button.
- On restart, pending confirmation views are re-registered in [payment_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_cog.py#L166).
- So the main failure mode is not timeout. It’s things like:
  - the message being deleted
  - the destination channel/thread no longer existing
  - or us wanting free-text confirmation instead of button-only

5. Free-text confirmation with recovery
- This is the part that is not built yet, and it needs real persistence.
- The robust design is:
  - keep the button as one valid confirmation path
  - add free-text as a second valid confirmation path
  - gate free-text by exact `recipient_user_id`, exact `channel/thread`, and exact active intent/payment
  - only then run an LLM classifier on that message
  - ambiguous result does nothing
- For restart recovery, do not rely on “what messages have I read in memory.”
- Add persisted intent fields like:
  - `awaiting_wallet_since_message_id`
  - `awaiting_confirmation_since_message_id`
  - `last_scanned_message_id`
  - `resolved_by_message_id`
- Then on startup or periodic reconciliation:
  - fetch channel/thread history after `last_scanned_message_id`
  - only inspect messages from the expected recipient
  - process in chronological order
  - persist the newest scanned message id as you go
- That gives you real offline catch-up instead of hoping the live event fired.

6. Secure wallet storage
- Current wallet storage is already reasonably locked down:
  - canonical wallet table in [payments.sql](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/sql/payments.sql#L13)
  - RLS enabled and broad access revoked in [payments.sql](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/sql/payments.sql#L192)
  - wallet verification tied to test-payment success in [payment_service.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_service.py#L339) and [db_handler.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/common/db_handler.py#L1547)
- So the storage side is in decent shape already.

My recommendation is:
- make `SOL` the canonical amount for the new admin flow
- allow deterministic wallet extraction from replied-to messages
- keep button confirmation
- add free-text confirmation as a second path
- add persisted message cursors so recovery works if the bot is down

If you want, I can turn that into the next megaplan revision in exact implementation terms: schema changes, intent states, message-cursor recovery, and the confirmation-classifier contract.

> AGENT

Yes. I’d revise the plan in three specific ways from the current `plan_v2`:

- make `SOL` the canonical amount field, not `USD`
- persist admin payment intents in DB, not in memory
- add restart-safe free-text confirmation recovery, not button-only confirmation

**Exact Revision**
Add a new table, `admin_payment_intents`, with fields like:
- `intent_id`
- `guild_id`
- `source_channel_id`
- `source_thread_id`
- `source_message_id`
- `admin_user_id`
- `recipient_user_id`
- `requested_amount_sol`
- `reason`
- `wallet_id`
- `wallet_address_snapshot`
- `status`
- `test_payment_id`
- `final_payment_id`
- `wallet_prompt_message_id`
- `confirmation_prompt_message_id`
- `last_scanned_message_id`
- `resolved_by_message_id`
- `last_error`
- `metadata`
- `created_at`, `updated_at`

Statuses should be:
- `pending_wallet`
- `wallet_received`
- `test_payment_requested`
- `awaiting_test_result`
- `awaiting_final_confirmation`
- `final_payment_requested`
- `completed`
- `manual_hold`
- `failed`
- `cancelled`

The admin tool becomes:
- `initiate_payment(recipient_user_id, amount_sol, reason?)`
- guild channel/thread only
- no wallet param
- if the admin message is a reply, deterministically inspect the replied-to message for exactly one valid Solana address before asking the recipient
- if a verified wallet already exists, use it directly
- otherwise create the intent row and ping the recipient in the same channel/thread to reply with their wallet

**Wallet / Thread Handling**
The bot should use deterministic handling, not just LLM interpretation:
- read `channel_context.replied_to` if the admin replied to a wallet message
- parse that message for a valid Solana address
- if exactly one valid address is found, use it
- if not, check stored wallet
- if still none, ask the intended recipient in-thread

For recipient wallet replies:
- add a dedicated listener ahead of normal admin-chat permission gating
- only accept messages from the exact `recipient_user_id`
- only in the exact `source_channel_id` / `source_thread_id`
- validate Solana address format
- upsert into `wallet_registry`
- then create the fixed tiny test payment

**SOL Amount Semantics**
For this flow:
- store `requested_amount_sol` as the canonical amount
- create final payment with that exact SOL amount
- optionally store `amount_usd_reference` in metadata if you want reporting context
- stop treating USD as the source of truth for admin-triggered payments

That means `PaymentService.request_payment(...)` needs a second non-test path:
- either `amount_token` for exact-token payouts
- or `amount_usd` for converted payouts
- but not both required

**Confirmation**
Keep both paths:
- button confirmation
- free-text confirmation

Button path:
- stays persistent with `timeout=None`
- only intended recipient can press it
- button is disabled after valid press, not deleted

Free-text path:
- only inspect messages from the intended recipient
- only inspect them in the intended channel/thread
- only while the intent is in `awaiting_final_confirmation`
- run an LLM classifier only after those hard gates
- classifier outputs:
  - `confirmed_received`
  - `not_confirmed`
  - `ambiguous`
- only `confirmed_received` advances
- everything else stays pending or moves to `manual_hold`

**Recovery**
This is the important new piece.

On startup and periodically:
- load all `admin_payment_intents` in `pending_wallet` or `awaiting_final_confirmation`
- fetch message history in the source channel/thread after `last_scanned_message_id`
- process only messages from the expected recipient
- in chronological order
- for each relevant message:
  - if waiting for wallet, try wallet validation
  - if waiting for final confirmation, run confirmation classification
- persist `last_scanned_message_id` as you go
- persist `resolved_by_message_id` when a wallet or confirmation is accepted

That gives you real offline catch-up. It does not rely on in-memory “which messages have I seen.”

**Permissions**
- Admin can create/cancel/inspect intents.
- Recipient can only:
  - provide wallet for their own open intent
  - confirm their own open intent
- Other users cannot progress the flow.
- Member permission bypass is narrow:
  - only for an open intent
  - only for the expected recipient
  - only in the expected channel/thread

**What I’d Change In The Current Megaplan**
The current [plan_v2.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/.megaplan/plans/admin-triggered-payments-let-20260410-1448/plan_v2.md) is better than v1, but I would revise these parts:
- replace in-memory intent store with DB-backed `admin_payment_intents`
- replace `amount_usd` with `amount_sol`
- replace button-only final confirmation with button + restart-safe free-text recovery
- add message-cursor recovery fields and reconciliation job
- add deterministic replied-to wallet extraction

**Main Concern**
The only truly sharp edge here is free-text financial confirmation. It is acceptable only if:
- identity is hard-gated first
- channel/thread is hard-gated first
- active intent is hard-gated first
- ambiguity never advances payout

If you want, I can push the megaplan one more iteration around exactly these changes so the artifact matches this revised design instead of the current `plan_v2`.

> DEVELOPER

Okay, yeah so on that then I think what you suggest makes sense across the board and I think on the identity being hard gated I think that makes sense too. I think one of the things is that the thread should obviously work with multiple people. So multiple people are the channel to be able to post or add into the thread or channel, you know, confirm their own payment thing respectively. And we should have the agents actually like confirming it as well properly. So and then executing the next step. So that's the agent should probably have the tools and be the gatekeeper for this. But obviously we should be having maybe identity gated first.

> AGENT

Yes. That’s the right split.

The safe design is:

1. Deterministic gate first
- Match by `guild_id + channel/thread_id + recipient_user_id + active intent status`
- That lets multiple people use the same thread/channel at once, because each open intent is tied to a specific recipient
- A message only becomes a candidate if it came from the exact intended user for one of the open intents in that thread/channel

2. Agent classification second
- Only after a message passes the identity/context gate do we let the agent classify it
- The agent decides things like:
  - “this is a wallet address”
  - “this is a positive confirmation”
  - “this is ambiguous / not a confirmation”
- The agent should not decide who the user is, which intent they belong to, or whether they’re allowed to advance the flow

3. Agent executes next step
- Once the message is matched to a valid intent and classified:
  - wallet message -> upsert wallet -> queue test payment
  - positive confirmation -> queue final payment
  - ambiguous -> leave pending
- So yes, the agent can be the semantic gatekeeper, but only inside a hard deterministic envelope

For multiple people in one thread/channel:
- That is fine if intents are keyed individually
- Example key shape:
  - `intent_id`
  - `guild_id`
  - `source_channel_id`
  - `source_thread_id`
  - `recipient_user_id`
  - `status`
- Then if Alice and Bob both have pending payments in the same thread:
  - Alice’s message can only affect Alice’s intent
  - Bob’s message can only affect Bob’s intent

What I would not do:
- I would not let the agent scan all thread messages loosely and infer which payment to advance
- I would not let the agent resolve identity from text alone
- I would not let ambiguous confirmations auto-advance

So the control model should be:
- deterministic matcher picks the candidate intent
- agent classifies the matched message
- payment service executes the state transition
- ambiguous cases stay pending or go `manual_hold`

That is the architecture I’d lock into the megaplan revision. If you want, I can now update the plan around exactly that model and then move it toward a final execution-ready artifact.

I also patched the admin-payment flow so the initiating admin is stored on the intent and gets a DM when the test payment confirms and when the final payment confirms. The relevant code is in [agent.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/agent.py), [tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/tools.py), and [admin_chat_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/admin_chat_cog.py). If an intent somehow lacks `admin_user_id`, it falls back to `ADMIN_USER_ID`.

> AGENT

This is the issue: both new cogs block inside `cog_load()` on `await bot.wait_until_ready()`, but `cog_load()` runs during `await bot.add_cog(...)` before `bot.start(...)` ever happens. That can deadlock startup indefinitely, which leaves `/ready` stuck at 503 forever. I’m patching that now so the startup work runs from `on_ready` / background sync instead of blocking cog registration.

> AGENT

The healthcheck was failing because the bot never finished startup, so `/ready` stayed at `503 not_ready`.

Root cause:
- [payment_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_cog.py) had `await self.bot.wait_until_ready()` inside `cog_load()`
- [admin_chat_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/admin_chat_cog.py) had the same pattern
- those `cog_load()` methods run during `await bot.add_cog(...)` in [main.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/main.py), before `bot.start(...)` can complete
- so startup deadlocked before Discord `on_ready` ever fired
- health server was up, but [health_server.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/common/health_server.py) only marks `/ready` healthy after bot ready, so Railway kept seeing `service unavailable`

I fixed it by moving that startup sync to non-blocking ready-time behavior:
- `PaymentCog` now starts its worker in `cog_load()` without waiting, and does view recovery/inflight recovery from `on_ready`
- `AdminChatCog` now reconciles intents from `on_ready` instead of blocking `cog_load()`

Pushed fix:
- commit `545a715`
- already pushed to `origin/main`

Verification:
- `PYTHONPATH=. PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 pytest -q tests/test_admin_payments.py tests/test_scheduler.py`
- `25 passed`

One note: `a04922` is not a git commit in this repo, so I treated it as deploy context, not a revision. The deploy should be retried from `main` at `545a715`.

Put confirm/notify routing details in `route_config`, for example `{\"use_source_thread\": true}` or explicit channel/thread IDs.\n@@\n **Never show raw errors.** If a tool fails, do NOT paste the error message. Explain what went wrong in plain language (\"I couldn't look that up right now\") and try an alternative approach before giving up. If all approaches fail, say so simply without technical details.\n+\n+**Use the right route tools.** Social routes are only for outbound posting to social accounts and require an `account` in `route_config`. > AGENT

I fixed this locally.

Most likely cause: the admin agent was mixing up `social routes` and `payment routes`. Social routes for Twitter require `route_config.account`, so a mistaken social-route call could bubble up as the raw `'"account"'` error.

What I changed:
- Social-route creation/update now validates `route_config.account` and, if it’s missing, returns a useful message that points the agent toward `create_payment_route` instead: [tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/tools.py#L999), [tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/tools.py#L2076)
- The admin agent prompt now explicitly says payment routing, confirmations, wallets, and payouts must use payment tools, not social-route tools: [agent.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/agent.py#L79)
- Unexpected admin-chat exceptions no longer leak raw strings like `Error: '"account"'` back into Discord: [agent.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/agent.py#L485)

I added regressions for this and the targeted suite passes:
- [test_social_route_tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/tests/test_social_route_tools.py#L345)
- `PYTHONPATH=. PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 pytest -q tests/test_social_route_tools.py`
- Result: `8 passed`

I haven’t pushed or deployed this fix yet. If you want, I can push it now.

> AGENT

Yes. The live `system_logs` show the exact cause.

What’s happening:
- The bot is crashing before any tool call.
- The failure is in [agent.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/agent.py#L348) while building the admin system prompt.
- The prompt contains literal JSON examples like `{"account": "main"}`.
- We were using `prompt_template.format(...)`, so Python treated `"account"` as a format field and raised `KeyError: '"account"'`.

Live log evidence from `system_logs`:
- `2026-04-10 16:48:35 ERROR [AdminChat] Unexpected error: '"account"'`
- traceback points to `system = prompt_template.format(...)`
- same crash happened for both `can you make payments/` and plain `hi`

I fixed it by replacing `.format(...)` with a safe placeholder renderer that only substitutes the known prompt tokens and leaves JSON braces alone:
- [agent.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/agent.py#L25)
- regression added in [test_social_route_tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/tests/test_social_route_tools.py#L376)

Verification:
- `PYTHONPATH=. PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 pytest -q tests/test_social_route_tools.py`
- `9 passed`

Pushed:
- commit `40b1814`
- branch `main`

So the root cause was not payment routing logic. It was prompt rendering choking on the literal `{"account": "main"}` example in the admin prompt.

Yes. I checked the live `system_logs`, and the exact failure is now clear.

It was not a payment-route runtime error. It was the admin-chat prompt crashing before any tool call:

- live log: `2026-04-10 16:48:35 ERROR [AdminChat] Unexpected error: '"account"'`
- traceback: [agent.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/agent.py#L348)
- failing line was `prompt_template.format(...)`

Root cause:
- the admin system prompt includes literal JSON examples like `{"account": "main"}`
- Python `.format(...)` interpreted `"account"` as a format placeholder
- that raised `KeyError: '"account"'`
- so even a DM like `hi` crashed before the agent could respond

I fixed it by replacing `.format(...)` with a safe placeholder renderer that only substitutes the known prompt fields and leaves JSON braces untouched:
- [agent.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/agent.py#L25)
- regression test: [test_social_route_tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/tests/test_social_route_tools.py#L376)

Verification:
- `PYTHONPATH=. PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 pytest -q tests/test_social_route_tools.py`
- `9 passed`

Pushed:
- commit `40b1814`
- branch `main`

So the next step is just to redeploy `main`. After that, the admin chat should stop failing on simple messages.

> AGENT

Not as-written, no. There is no explicit “send full wallet balance” code path, but there is one real high-risk edge case:

1. High: the admin-triggered flow has no hard max amount cap, so if the agent/tool call creates a huge `amount_sol`, the system will try to send that exact amount if the hot wallet has enough balance. The amount is accepted directly in [tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/tools.py#L2437) and then used verbatim for the final payout in [admin_chat_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/admin_chat_cog.py#L352). The Solana provider only checks “amount > 0, valid address, enough balance” in [solana_provider.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/solana_provider.py#L66). So it won’t accidentally compute “all funds,” but it absolutely could send nearly all funds if a bad amount gets queued.

2. Medium: the admin flow can reuse an already verified wallet on file without re-collecting it. That is convenient, but if the stored wallet is stale/wrong, the test payment goes there automatically in [admin_chat_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/admin_chat_cog.py#L225). The full payout still won’t go unless the intended recipient confirms, so this is not a “drain the wallet” bug, but it is a “small amount can go to the wrong wallet” risk.

3. Medium: free-text confirmation is identity-gated, which is good, but it still relies on LLM classification for semantics in [admin_chat_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/admin_chat_cog.py#L599). That means the intended recipient could send an ambiguous message that gets misread as positive confirmation. The design fails closed on many ambiguities, but this is still softer than button-only confirmation.

What is already safe:
- wrong user cannot confirm a payment; the service enforces recipient identity in [payment_service.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_service.py#L176)
- ambiguous post-submit states go `manual_hold`, not resend
- there is no admin path to mark `manual_hold` straight to `confirmed`
- there is no “send everything in the wallet” function

My blunt recommendation:
- add a hard cap, for example `MAX_ADMIN_PAYMENT_SOL`
- require an extra admin confirmation step above a threshold
- keep button confirmation as primary and free-text as fallback
- optionally require the admin tool to echo back `recipient + amount` and wait for a second explicit admin “yes” before creating any payout above a threshold

So the answer is:
- no automatic “send all funds” bug that I can see
- yes, a too-large `amount_sol` could still move a very large amount, because there is currently no hard upper bound

If you want, I can patch the cap and two-step approval next.

> DEVELOPER

nah, can you share a quick sumamry of this part of the app?

> AGENT

The payments part is now a shared Solana payout subsystem used by grants and admin-triggered payouts.

At a high level:
- `PaymentService` creates payment ledger rows, confirms them, executes them, and recovers safely after restarts: [payment_service.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/payment_service.py)
- `SolanaProvider` is the chain adapter that validates wallet/address/balance, submits the SOL transfer, and confirms chain status: [solana_provider.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/payments/solana_provider.py)
- The database holds three main payment-side tables:
  - `wallet_registry`
  - `payment_channel_routes`
  - `payment_requests`
  in [payments.sql](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/sql/payments.sql)

How the flow works:
- A producer creates a payment request.
- The payment starts in `pending_confirmation`.
- Once the intended recipient confirms, it moves to `queued`, then `processing`, then `submitted`, then either `confirmed`, `failed`, or `manual_hold`.
- If submission or confirmation becomes ambiguous, it fails closed into `manual_hold` instead of retrying blindly.

Current producers:
- `GrantsCog` uses it for ArcCompute microgrants: [grants_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/grants/grants_cog.py)
- `AdminChatCog` uses it for admin-triggered payouts with stored-wallet or wallet-collection flow: [admin_chat_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/admin_chat_cog.py)

Admin-triggered payout flow:
- Admin says to pay a user an amount in SOL.
- If the user already has a verified wallet, the bot uses it.
- Otherwise the bot asks that exact user for a wallet in-channel.
- Bot sends a tiny fixed test payment first.
- After the test confirms, bot asks the recipient to confirm the final payout.
- Then it queues the real payment.

Safety properties:
- no auto-resend after ambiguous submission
- recipient identity is enforced on confirmation
- payment and wallet tables are excluded from broad admin DB querying
- manual review path exists via `manual_hold`

Weakest current spot:
- admin-triggered payouts do not yet have a hard max-amount cap, so large amounts rely on the admin/agent getting the number right.
